```text
Both the code and config already exist. Let me verify:

1. `train_grpo.py` has `additive_random` as a valid reward_mode
2. The `additive_random` branch in `make_reward_fn` does: `r = execution_reward * (1 + beta * rand_val)` — exactly what's needed
3. `grpo_additive_random.yaml` config mirrors `grpo_additive_bonus.yaml` exactly, with only:
   - `reward_mode: additive_random` (vs `additive_bonus`)
   - `output_dir: /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan017_additive_random_s42` (vs `plan002_additive_bonus_s42`)
   - `wandb_run: plan017_additive_random_b03` (vs `additive_bonus_b03`)

All other hyperparams are identical: same model, data, num_steps=500, batch_size=4, grad_accum=2, num_generations=8, max_completion_length=512, lr=1e-6, beta=0.3, seed=42, solver=prover9.

The random value is sampled per-sample, with each completion getting its own seed derived from the `stats["total"]` counter when iterating through the reward function.

Now I need to verify that the LocalLogCallback is actually capturing the random_reward value in the logs...

Checking the logging code...

I see it's tracking `isr_mean` from step_stats, but I'm not seeing where random_reward itself gets logged. The value gets stored in `result["random_reward"]` during the additive_random branch, but it doesn't appear to be aggregated into the step statistics or the reward log. Meanwhile, the ISR is still being computed from the actual STA reward even in random mode, which is useful for analysis since you can observe the real ISR values even though they're not driving the training. But I need to trace through where random_reward actually gets tracked in the stats pipeline.
```

The random field is being logged correctly in the reward calculation, and I can see the ISR values are tracked separately even though they're not directly used in the reward computation. The reward formula appears to be working as intended — in step 1 with a prove_rate of 0.5, the reward calculation checks out, while step 2 shows zero reward when nothing was proved.

Now I'm realizing the key insight: even when execution_reward is zero, the random value is still being sampled and logged because the random tracking happens independently of whether that randomness actually affects the final reward. The code generates the random value regardless, so it appears in the logs even when multiplying by zero execution_reward makes it irrelevant to the actual reward. So the math checks out — when execution_reward is 0, multiplying by the random factor still gives 0, which is exactly what we're seeing in step 2.
